## Indian Oil and Gas Regulations {#indian-oil-and-gas-regulations .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/2019-196
:::

[INDIAN OIL AND GAS ACT](/eng/acts/I-7)

Registration 2019-06-10

Indian Oil and Gas Regulations

P.C. 2019-755 2019-06-09
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister of Indian Affairs and Northern Development, pursuant to
section 4.1[[Footnote ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_a){#fn_a-ID0EIADA} and
subsection 21(1)[[Footnote ]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#fn_b){#fn_b-ID0EGADA}
of the [Indian Oil and Gas Act](/eng/acts/I-7)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^c^](#fn_c){#fn_c-ID0EDADA}, makes the annexed [Indian
Oil and Gas Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2019-196).

  [[Return to footnote ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_a-ID0EIADA){#fn_a}S.C.
  2009, c. 7, s. 1

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1139566 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in these Regulations.

  [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Indian Oil and Gas
      Act](/eng/acts/I-7). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[actual selling price]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[actual selling price]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of oil, the price at which the oil
        is sold; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of gas, the price or consideration
        payable that is specified in the gas sales contract, free of any
        fees or deductions other than transmission charges beyond the
        facility outlet. ([prix de vente réel]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[adjoining]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[adjoining]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to two spacing
      units, means touching at a common point, without regard to any
      road allowances between the spacing
      units. ([adjacentes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[bitumen]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bitumen]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means oil that does not flow to a
      well unless it is heated or diluted. ([bitume]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[exploration work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[exploration work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes mapping,
      surveying, examining geological, geophysical or geochemical data,
      test drilling and any other activities that are carried out by
      air, land or water and are related to the exploration for oil or
      gas. ([travaux d'exploration]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[external spacing unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[external spacing unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to a
      First Nation, means any spacing unit that is not a First Nation
      spacing unit of that First Nation. ([unité d'espacement
      externe]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[First Nation spacing unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[First Nation spacing unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a spacing
      unit in which 50% or more of the lands are First Nation lands of
      the same First Nation. ([unité d'espacement d'une première
      nation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[horizontal section]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[horizontal section]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the portion of a
      wellbore that has

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an angle of at least 80°, measured between the
        line extending from the initial point of penetration into the
        target zone to the end point of the wellbore in that zone and
        the line extending vertically downward from the initial point of
        penetration into that zone; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a minimum length of 100 m, measured from the
        initial point of penetration into the target zone to the end
        point of the wellbore in that zone. ([tronçon
        horizontal]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[horizontal well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[horizontal well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a well that has been
      approved as a horizontal well by the provincial authority or a
      well with a horizontal section that has been approved by the
      provincial authority. ([puits horizontal]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[offset period]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offset period]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the period established
      in accordance with subsection 93(4). ([délai de
      compensation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offset well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offset well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a well that is located
      in a First Nation spacing unit adjoining an external spacing unit
      in which a triggering well is located and that is producing from
      the same zone as the triggering well. ([puits de
      limite]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offset zone]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offset zone]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the zone from which a
      triggering well is producing. ([couche de
      compensation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[pool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[pool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a natural underground reservoir
      that contains or appears to contain an accumulation of oil or gas
      that is separate or appears to be separate from any other such
      accumulation. ([bassin]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by the
      Minister under subsection 5(1) of the Act. ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[productive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[productive]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means producing or capable of
      producing oil or gas in a quantity that would warrant incurring

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the costs of completion, in the case of a well
        that has been drilled but not completed; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the costs of production, in the case of a well
        that has been completed. ([productif]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[provincial authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[provincial authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the office,
      department or body that is authorized by law to make decisions,
      grant approvals, receive information or keep records respecting
      the exploration for, or the exploitation or conservation of, oil
      and gas in the province in which the relevant First Nation lands
      are located. ([autorité provinciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[service well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[service well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a well that is operated
      for observation or for the injection, storage or disposal of
      fluids. ([puits de service]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[spacing unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[spacing unit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area in a zone that
      is designated as a spacing unit, a spacing area, a drainage unit
      or other similar unit by the provincial authority. ([unité
      d'espacement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[subsurface contract]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[subsurface contract]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a permit or
      subsurface lease granted under the Act. ([contrat relatif au
      sous-sol]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[surface contract]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[surface contract]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a surface lease or
      right-of-way granted under the Act. ([contrat relatif au
      sol]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[surface rates]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[surface rates]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the amounts, referred
      to in subsections 73(2) and (3), that are to be paid by a surface
      contract holder. ([droits de surface]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[triggering well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[triggering well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a well that is
      producing from one or more external spacing units adjoining a
      First Nation spacing unit. ([puits déclencheur]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[unit agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[unit agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement that
      combines the rights or interests of all the holders of oil and gas
      rights or interests in all or part of a pool and that provides for
      the joint exploitation of the oil and gas and the payment of
      royalties based on an attribution of production rather than actual
      production, but does not include an agreement that attributes
      production from a well referred to in subsection 107(1). ([accord
      de mise en commun]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[well]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a well that is used for the
      exploitation of oil or gas and includes a vertical well, a
      deviated well and a horizontal well. ([puits]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[zone]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[zone]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a stratum of lands identified
      as a zone in accordance with the log data set out in Schedule 3 or
      4, as the case may be. ([couche]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A reference to a document that is incorporated by
  reference into these Regulations is a reference to the document as
  amended from time to time or, if the document no longer exists, to any
  successor to it that provides the same information.

## [General Rules]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1139603 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice, document or information

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  notice, document or information that is sent or submitted under these
  Regulations must be in paper or electronic form or published on the
  website of Petrinex or any successor to Petrinex.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Address for service

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A contract holder must, in the prescribed form,
  provide the Minister with their address for service and send him or
  her a notice of any change to that address.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed receipt --- paper form

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any notice, document or information that the Minister
  sends to a holder in paper form at their address for service is deemed
  to have been received by the holder four days after the day on which
  it is sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed receipt --- electronic form

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any notice, document or information that the Minister
  sends to a holder in electronic form at their latest address for
  service or publishes on the website of Petrinex or any successor to
  Petrinex is deemed to have been received by the holder on the day on
  which it is sent or published.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record search

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person may apply to the Minister for a record
  search of non-confidential, contractual documentation that is in the
  Minister's possession and stored in electronic form if the application
  is in the prescribed form and accompanied by the record search fee set
  out in Schedule 1.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** Despite any provision of
these Regulations, a person is not obliged to submit information to the
Minister that the Minister has stated is in his or her possession or is
available to him or her from another source such as Petrinex.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form not prescribed

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** When an application or
other information is required by these Regulations to be submitted in a
prescribed form but no form has been prescribed, the application or
information may be submitted in any form.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative format

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** When a notice, a document
or information is required by these Regulations to be submitted in a
specified format, the person required to submit it may use an
alternative format if the Minister states that he or she has the
capacity to read and use the information in that alternative format.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Eligibility

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** A person is eligible to be
granted a contract if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are a corporation that is authorized by the laws
  of the relevant province to carry on business in that province or an
  individual who has reached the age of majority in that province;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are not in default under subsection 111(5); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a corporation, neither it nor any of
  its directors, officers or agents or mandataries has been convicted of
  an offence under subsection 18(2) of the Act within two years before
  the date of the bid, in the case of a grant by public tender, or the
  date of the application, in the case of a negotiated contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holder's responsibility

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** A contract holder must
ensure that any requirement that is related to their contract and is
imposed by these Regulations on a person other than the holder is
satisfied.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability --- holders and persons with
working interest

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  contract holder and person with a working interest in a contract is
  absolutely liable for any damage to the environment that is caused by
  operations carried out under the contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability --- operators and licensees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every operator, well licensee, pipeline licensee and
  facility licensee is absolutely liable for any damage to the
  environment that is caused by operations they carry out under the
  contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insurance required

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  contract holder must obtain, and maintain during the term of the
  contract, an insurance policy that is adequate to cover all risks
  resulting from the operations to be carried out under the contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum coverage

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The insurance policy must provide the following
  minimum coverage:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} comprehensive general liability insurance that
    covers the risks of damage caused by operations carried out under
    the contract with an inclusive bodily injury, death and property
    damage limit of at least \$5,000,000 per occurrence, including
    occupier's liability or liability for damage caused by immovables,
    employer's liability, employer's contingent liability, contractual
    liability, contractor's protective liability, products liability,
    completed operations liability and contractor's liability insurance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} automobile liability insurance that covers all
    vehicles used in operations carried out under the contract with an
    inclusive bodily injury, death and property damage limit of at least
    \$5,000,000 per occurrence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if aircraft are to be used in operations carried
    out under the contract, aircraft liability insurance with an
    inclusive bodily injury, death and property damage limit of at least
    \$10,000,000 per occurrence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subrogation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every insurance policy obtained by the holder must
  provide that the insurer's right of subrogation is waived in favour of
  the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of cancellation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The holder must send the Minister notice without
  delay if any coverage under their insurance policy is terminated and
  at least 30 days before the last day of coverage if the holder intends
  to cancel any of their coverage.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum deductible

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The deductible of every insurance policy must not
  exceed 5% of the amount of insurance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Self-insurance

**[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** A holder may satisfy the
requirement imposed by subsection 9(1) by providing the Minister with a
letter of self-insurance in the prescribed form in which the holder

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} acknowledges liability for any damage caused by
  operations carried out under the contract; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} declares that their financial resources are adequate
  to cover that liability.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contractor's insurance

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** A contract holder must
ensure that any person that carries out operations under the contract,
other than an employee, obtains and maintains an insurance policy that
is adequate to cover all risks resulting from those operations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contract area boundaries

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  boundaries of a contract area must correspond to the boundaries of the
  legal land divisions of the relevant province if the lands in the
  contract area have been surveyed, or to the anticipated boundaries of
  those divisions if the lands have not been surveyed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unsurveyed lands

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the lands in a contract area are surveyed during
  the term of the contract, the Minister must, after consulting with the
  holder and the council, amend the contract so that the description of
  the contract area complies with subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply if the lands in
  the contract area are First Nation lands whose configuration prevents
  compliance with those subsections.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Survey plan

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  survey plan that is required under these Regulations must be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} plotted in accordance with the [Canada Lands
    Surveys Act](/eng/acts/L-6);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} approved by the Surveyor General of Canada; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} recorded in the Canada Lands Survey Records.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an exploration work survey plan; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a survey of lands under a treaty land entitlement
    agreement or a specific claim settlement agreement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dispute

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** If a dispute arises
regarding the location of a well, facility or boundary referred to in a
contract, the Minister may order the contract holder to have a survey
carried out as soon as the circumstances permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual meeting request

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  council whose First Nation lands are subject to a contract may, no
  more than once a year, submit a request to the Minister in the
  prescribed form for a meeting with the contract holder for the purpose
  of discussing the operations that have been carried out, or are
  planned to be carried out, in the contract area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the holder notice of a meeting
  request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrangement of meeting

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The holder must organize the meeting and ensure that
  it takes place within 90 days after the day on which the Minister's
  notice is received. In the case of multiple holders, they may
  designate one of their number to attend as their representative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple contracts

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the holder has more than one contract in the First
  Nation lands, operations carried out under all the contracts may be
  discussed at the same meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any expense relating to the request for, preparation
  for or attendance at a meeting must be borne by the party that incurs
  the expense.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unforeseen incident

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** An operator must, in the
most expeditious manner possible, notify the Minister and the council of
any unforeseen incident that occurs during operations carried out under
a contract and that results, or could result, in bodily injury or death
or in damage to First Nation lands or property. The operator must report
the details of the incident, in the prescribed form, as soon as the
circumstances permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person accompanying inspector

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
monitoring compliance with the Act and these Regulations, a person may
accompany an inspector who is inspecting a contract holder's facilities
and operations on First Nation lands if the person is authorized to do
so by a written resolution of the council and the person has the
certifications, and complies with the occupational health and safety
requirements, required or imposed by the holder or by law.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of rent

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  annual rent that is payable under a contract must be paid on or before
  the anniversary of the effective date of the contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refund

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The rent that is payable for the year in which a
  contract ends must be paid and is not refundable. However, any rent
  that has been paid for a subsequent year must be refunded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a contract that
  provides otherwise and was granted before the day on which these
  Regulations came into force.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment to Receiver General

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  money that is owed to Her Majesty under these Regulations or a
  contract must be paid to the Receiver General for Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of payment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The money must be accompanied by a statement, in the
  prescribed form, indicating the purpose for which it is paid.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  amendment to a contract or a bitumen recovery project requires the
  prior approval of the council and the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limits

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must not approve an amendment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an additional bonus is paid, if necessary, to
    reflect the fair value, determined in accordance with section 38, of
    the rights or interests granted by the amendment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} additional surface rates are paid, if necessary, in
    accordance with subsections 73(2) and (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an amendment
  referred to in subsection 12(2) or to one that reduces the area of
  lands that are subject to a subsurface contract or a bitumen recovery
  project.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Well data

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** An operator that carries
out operations in connection with a well must submit the following
documents and information to the Minister and the council within the
following time limits:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} before the day on which the well is spudded,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the provincial licence authorizing the
    drilling of the well and a copy of the licence application,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the drilling and coring plan proposed for the
    well,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the geological prognosis,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any proposed horizontal drilling plan, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the surface lease survey plan;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the day on which the well is
  rig-released,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all daily drilling reports for the period beginning
    on the day on which the rig move begins and ending on the day of
    rig-release,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a copy of each wireline log prepared,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the results of any drill-stem test conducted,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the final downhole well drilling survey,
    if one is required by the provincial authority,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any description, test or analysis resulting from an
    identification of any well sections that were cored, and

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a copy of the geological report, if one is
    required by the provincial authority;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the day on which the well is
  completed,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all daily completion reports and the final downhole
    well schematic,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a copy of each wireline log prepared,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any core and fluid analyses prepared,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any swab reports prepared,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the results of any pressure or flow tests
    conducted, including the results of any surface casing vent flow
    test,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a hydraulic fracturing fluid component information
    disclosure report, and

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a detailed report of any downhole well
    intervention or stimulation;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the day on which any
  recompletion or workover of the well is completed,

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all daily recompletion or workover reports,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a copy of each wireline log prepared,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any core and fluid analyses prepared,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any swab reports prepared,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the results of any pressure or flow tests
    conducted, including the results of any surface casing vent flow
    test,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a hydraulic fracturing fluid component information
    disclosure report,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a detailed report of any downhole well
    intervention or stimulation, and

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the final downhole well schematic;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the day on which the well is
  downhole-abandoned, all daily operation reports relating to the
  downhole abandonment; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the day on which the well is
  surface-abandoned, all daily operations reports of the cut and cap
  operation and a copy of the final abandonment report submitted to the
  provincial authority.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The operator must submit
to the Minister and the council any additional technical information
about the well that is necessary to determine its productivity.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential information

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  information that is submitted to the Minister or a council under the
  Act must be kept confidential until the end of the period in which
  such information must be kept confidential under the laws of the
  relevant province, unless the person that submitted it consents in
  writing to its disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seismic data

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), seismic data submitted by an
  exploration licence holder under paragraph 33(3)(a) may be disclosed
  by the Minister or the council on the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the holder also holds a subsurface lease or
    permit in lands in the licence area, the day on which the lease
    expires or is continued, the initial term of the permit expires or,
    in the case of a permit issued under the [Indian Oil and Gas
    Regulations, 1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753), the permit is
    converted to one or more leases, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fifth anniversary of the day on which the
    exploration work is completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any interpretation of seismic data, including maps,
  that is submitted to the Minister or a council under the Act may be
  disclosed only if the person that submitted it consents in writing to
  its disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure to council

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) to (3), the Minister may at
  any time disclose

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confidential information to a council if required
    to do so by the Act, any regulations made under the Act or a
    contract; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the results of an environmental review referred to
    in subsection 29(3), 57(2) or 75(2) to a council or the public.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorrect information

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** A person that submits
information to the Minister and becomes aware that it is incorrect must
submit the correct information to the Minister as soon as the
circumstances permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of assignment

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  assignment of any of the rights or interests conferred by a contract
  must be approved by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meeting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before the application for approval is submitted to
  the Minister, the assignee must meet with the council unless the
  council waives the meeting. The meeting must be face to face, unless
  the parties agree to another mode of meeting.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any expense relating to the request for, preparation
  for or attendance at a meeting must be borne by the party that incurs
  the expense.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for approval

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The application for approval must be in the
  prescribed form and include a statement by the assignee that a meeting
  with the council took place or that the council waived the meeting.
  The application must be accompanied by the assignment approval
  application fee set out in Schedule 1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to council

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The applicant must send the council a copy of the
  application for approval on or before the day on which the application
  is submitted to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to approve

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must not approve the assignment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is conditional;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it would result in more than five persons having a
    right or interest in the contract;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it assigns an undivided right or interest in the
    contract that is less than 1%;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it divides the oil and gas rights or interests
    conferred by the contract;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the assignee is not eligible under section 6;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the assignment was not signed by the assignor and
    assignee; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the assignee fails to establish that they have the
    financial ability to fulfill the assignor's obligations under the
    Act with respect to remediation and reclamation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's decision

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister approves the assignment and signs it,
  he or she must send a copy to the assignor and assignee and a notice
  of the approval to the council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The assignment takes effect on the day on which the
  Minister approves it unless it provides for a different effective day.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  assignment is approved, the assignor and assignee are jointly and
  severally, or solidarily, liable for any obligation owing and any
  liability arising under the contract before the day on which the
  assignment is approved, even if the contract is subsequently assigned.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an assignment that
  is approved before the coming into force of these Regulations.

## [Terms and Conditions To Be Included in Every Contract]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1139788 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with laws

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  contract granted by the Minister under these Regulations includes the
  holder's undertaking to comply with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5), and any orders
    made under that Act, as amended from time to time;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Act, and any regulations or orders made under
    the Act, as amended from time to time; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the laws of the relevant province, as amended from
    time to time, that relate to the environment or to the exploration
    for, or the exploitation, treatment, processing or conservation of,
    oil and gas, including equitable production, if those laws are not
    in conflict with the Act or any regulations or orders made under the
    Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency --- Acts, regulations and
  orders

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The provisions of any Act, regulation or order
  referred to in subsection (1) prevail over any terms and conditions of
  the contract, except for any terms and conditions respecting royalties
  that are the subject of a special agreement under subsection 4(2) of
  the Act, to the extent of any inconsistency. The provisions of any Act
  of Parliament, or any regulation or order made under an Act of
  Parliament, referred to in subsection (1) prevail over the laws of the
  province referred to in subsection (1), to the extent of any
  inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistency --- interpretation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, provisions ---
  whether legislative or contractual --- are not inconsistent unless it
  is impossible for the holder to comply with both.

## [Exploration]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1139799 .Part}

### [Authorization]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1139800 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization to explore

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** A person may carry out
exploration work on First Nation lands if they

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} hold an exploration licence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} have obtained from the provincial authority any
  approval that is required to carry out exploration work in the
  province; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} are in compliance with the terms and conditions of
  the licence and the approval.

### [Application for Exploration Licence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1139806 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary negotiation

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before
  applying for an exploration licence, an applicant and the council must
  agree on the location of the proposed seismic lines and on the seismic
  rates, if those rates have not already been fixed in a related
  subsurface contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for exploration licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must be submitted to the Minister in
  the prescribed form and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions negotiated with the
    council;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the approval of the provincial authority is
    required to carry out exploration work, a statement that the
    approval has been received;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the proposed exploration program,
    including the licence area, the exploration work to be carried out,
    the equipment to be used, the name of the geophysical contractor to
    be engaged and the anticipated duration of the work;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the results of an environmental review of the
    proposed exploration program that has been conducted by a qualified
    environmental professional who deals with the applicant at arm's
    length; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the exploration licence application fee set out in
    Schedule 1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental review

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The results of the environmental review must be
  submitted in the prescribed form and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a site evaluation that is based on the site's
    topography, soils, vegetation, wildlife, sources of water, existing
    structures, archeological and cultural resources, traditional
    ecological knowledge, current land uses and any other feature of the
    site that could be affected by the proposed exploration program;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the operations to be carried out
    during the proposed exploration program, the duration of each and
    its location on the site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the short-term and long-term
    effects that each operation could have on the environment of the
    site and on any surrounding areas;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the proposed mitigation measures,
    the potential residual effects after mitigation and the significance
    of those effects; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of the consultations undertaken with
    the council and the First Nation members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental protection measures

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the exploration program can be carried out without
  causing irremediable damage to the First Nation lands, the Minister
  must send the application to the applicant and the council, along with
  a letter that sets out the environmental protection measures that must
  be implemented to permit the licence holder to carry out their
  exploration program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of documents

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} To obtain the exploration licence, the applicant
  must, within 90 days after the day on which the reviewed application
  is received, submit to the Minister three copies of the environmental
  protection measures letter and three original copies of the
  application signed by the applicant, along with a written resolution
  of the council approving the licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exploration licence

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the requirements set out in this section are met,
  the Minister must grant the exploration licence for a period of one
  year. The terms and conditions of the licence are those set out in the
  application and the environmental protection measures letter. The
  licence takes effect on the day on which it is signed by the Minister.

### [Operations Under Exploration Licence]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1139830 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of rights conferred by licence

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** An exploration licence
holder may exercise the rights conferred by the licence in a subsurface
contract area, but in doing so must not interfere with any operations
carried out under the subsurface contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Priority

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** Every exploration licence
is subject to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any surface rights or interests granted under an Act
  of Parliament; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any rights or interests related to the exploration or
  exploitation of minerals other than oil or gas in the licence area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Maximum drilling depth

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  exploration licence holder must not drill to a depth of more than 50
  m, unless authorized to do so by their licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Holder's obligations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The holder must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that all environmental protection measures
    included in the licence are implemented and complied with;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} identify and mark the location of every test hole
    and shot hole that is drilled under the licence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} repair and recondition any roads or road allowances
    that are damaged as a result of the exploration work as soon as the
    circumstances permit after the damage occurs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} as soon as the circumstances permit, plug any hole
    that is drilled under the licence and that, during or after
    completion of the exploration work, collapses or emits gas, water or
    another substance;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} within 90 days after the day on which the
    exploration work is completed, pay compensation for the exploration
    work that was carried out, based on the rates specified in the
    licence or a related subsurface contract; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} within 90 days after the day on which the
    exploration work is completed, submit to the Minister and the
    council

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a mylar sepia copy and a legible paper copy of a
      map, on a scale of not less than 1:50 000, that shows the location
      and ground elevation of every vibrating equipment station, test
      hole and shot hole,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} summaries of any geologist's and driller's logs,
      indicating the depth and thickness of formations bearing water,
      sand, gravel, coal and other minerals of possible economic value,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} all technical information obtained from the
      drilling of each test hole.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exploration report

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  exploration licence holder must submit an exploration report to the
  Minister within 90 days after the day on which the exploration work is
  completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of exploration report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report must comply with any exploration reporting
  requirements of the relevant province and must include, in addition to
  the documents and information referred to in paragraph 32(2)(f),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a copy of every aerial photograph taken during the
    period of exploration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two copies of a geological report on the explored
    area, including stratigraphic data and structural and isopach maps
    on a scale of not less than 1:50 000; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a geophysical report on the explored area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of geophysical report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The geophysical report must include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if seismic work has been carried out,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a mylar sepia copy and two legible paper copies
      of a map, on a scale of not less than 1:50 000, that shows contour
      lines drawn on the corrected time value at each source point for
      all significant reflecting horizons explored, with a contour line
      interval of not more than 10 m,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a mylar sepia copy and two prefolded paper
      copies of each stacked seismic cross-section, including migrated
      displays if that process has been carried out, with all
      significant reflecting horizons clearly labelled at both ends on
      one of the copies, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} two microfilm copies of all basic recorded
      data, including survey notes, chaining notes and observer reports;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if a gravity survey has been carried out, two
    legible copies of a map, on a scale of not less than 1:50 000, that
    shows the location and ground elevation of each station, the final
    corrected gravity value at each station and gravity contour lines
    drawn on that value, with a contour line interval of not more than
    2.5 µm/s^2^; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if a magnetic survey has been carried out, two
    legible copies of a map of the explored area, on a scale of not less
    than 1:50 000, that shows the location of the flight lines or grid
    stations and magnetic contour lines, with a contour line interval of
    not more than 5 nT.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The holder may include maps at contour line intervals
  or scales other than those specified in subsections (2) and (3) if the
  alternative intervals or scales would enhance the interpretability of
  the maps.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information available to council

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must make the information submitted
  under subsections (2) to (4) available to the council.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information to be kept

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the information submitted under this
  section, the holder must keep any information that was obtained as a
  result of the exploration work carried out in the contract area,
  including any printout, or magnetic digital display, of raw seismic
  data or interpreted seismic data, and must make it available for
  review by the Minister at the holder's office during business hours
  after the later of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the holder also holds a subsurface lease or
    permit in lands in the licence area, 90 days after the day on which
    the lease expires or is continued, the initial term of the permit
    expires or, in the case of a permit issued under the [Indian Oil and
    Gas Regulations, 1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753), the permit is
    converted to one or more leases, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one year after the day on which the exploration
    work is completed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remediation and reclamation

**[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** When exploration work
under an exploration licence is no longer being carried out, whether or
not the licence has ended, the licence holder must ensure that all the
lands on which the work was carried out are remediated and reclaimed.

## [Subsurface Rights or Interests]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1139877 .Part}

### [Grants of Subsurface Rights or Interests]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1139878 .Subheading}

#### [General Rules]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1139879 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsurface contracts

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Oil
  and gas rights or interests in First Nation lands may be granted by
  the Minister under one of the following subsurface contracts:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an oil and gas permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an oil and gas lease.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Process

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A subsurface contract must be granted in accordance
  with the public tender process set out in sections 39 to 42 or the
  negotiation process set out in sections 44 to 46, as chosen by the
  council. The negotiation process may be preceded by a call for
  proposals in accordance with section 43.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No splitting of rights

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When granting a subsurface contract, the Minister
  must grant all the rights to the oil and gas in each zone included in
  the contract area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Priority

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** A subsurface contract
holder's rights or interests are subject to the right of an exploration
licence holder to carry out exploration work in, and the right of any
other subsurface contract holder to work through, the subsurface
contract area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple holders

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  subsurface contract may be granted to no more than five persons, each
  having an undivided right or interest in the contract of at least 1%.
  The right or interest must be expressed in decimal form to no more
  than seven decimal places.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If two or more persons have an undivided right or
  interest in a subsurface contract, they are jointly and severally, or
  solidarily, liable for all obligations under the contract, the Act and
  these Regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fair value

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** In determining the fair
value of the rights or interests to be granted under a subsurface
contract, the Minister must, in consultation with the council, consider
the bonuses paid for grants of oil and gas rights or interests in other
lands, which may be adjusted to take into account the following factors:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the size of the other lands and their proximity to
  the First Nation lands;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the time when the rights or interests in the other
  lands were granted;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} current oil and gas prices and the prices when the
  rights or interests were granted;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the results of recent drilling operations in the
  vicinity of the other lands;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} similarities and differences in the geological
  features of the other lands and the First Nation lands; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other factors that could affect the fair value of
  the rights or interests.

#### [Public Tender Process]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1139904 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public tender

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may grant
the oil and gas rights or interests in First Nation lands by way of
public tender only if the council requests or consents to that process.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's duties

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  oil and gas rights or interests are to be granted by way of public
  tender, the Minister must, after consulting with the council, prepare
  a notice of tender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of tender

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice of tender must include the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the type of subsurface contract to be granted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions of the contract, other
    than those set out in these Regulations, or the address of a website
    where the terms and conditions are set out, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands to be included in the
      contract area and the oil and gas rights or interests to be
      granted,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the surface rates and seismic rates,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the initial and intermediate terms of the
      permit or the term of the lease, as the case may be,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a permit, the earning provisions
      for the initial term, including the drilling commitment and
      deadline for completion, the target zone or depth to which each
      earning well must be drilled and a description of the lands to be
      earned by each, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the royalty to be paid, if it differs from the
      royalty provided for in these Regulations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the instructions for submitting a bid, including
    any information to be provided by bidders, the place where a bid may
    be submitted and the deadline for submission; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating that the bidder acknowledges
    that they have reviewed and understood the terms and conditions of
    the contract to be granted and will be bound by those terms and
    conditions if theirs is the winning bid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of notice of tender

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must submit a copy of the proposed
  notice of tender to the council before publishing it and, if it is
  approved, must publish it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a publication known to the industry, such as the
    Daily Oil Bulletin published by JuneWarren-Nickle's Energy Group; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on a website on which the Minister publishes
    information about oil and gas in First Nation lands.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of bids

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  bids must be submitted in accordance with the instructions set out in
  the notice of tender, be sealed and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the subsurface contract application fee set out in
    Schedule 1;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rent for the first year of the contract;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the bonus; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name and address for service of each proposed
    contract holder and the percentage share of each.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certified funds

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The fee, rent and bonus must be paid in certified
  funds unless the notice of tender specifies a different form of
  payment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Opening of bids

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After
  the tender closes, the Minister must without delay open the bids,
  exclude any bids that do not meet the requirements of section 41,
  identify the bid with the highest bonus and send the council notice of
  that bid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence at opening

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The council or a person designated by the council may
  be present when the Minister opens the bids.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tied bid

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the highest bonus is included in more than one
  bid, the Minister must republish the notice of tender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Council's decision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The council may, within 15 days after the day on
  which the tender closes, notify the Minister by written resolution
  that it rejects the bid with the highest bonus. If such a notice is
  received, all bids must be rejected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Irrevocable decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a council notifies the Minister that it approves
  the bid with the highest bonus, that bid cannot later be rejected
  under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance of highest bid

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a notice rejecting the bid is not received, the
  Minister must accept it and send the winning bidder a notice of
  acceptance. The contract takes effect on the day on which the tender
  closes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of tender results

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must publish the name of the winner and
  the winning bonus amount or, if no bid was accepted, a notice to that
  effect, in the publication or on the website where the notice of
  tender was published.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Except for the name of the winning bidder and bonus
  amount, the information in bids must be kept confidential.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contract granted

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must prepare the subsurface contract and
  send a copy to the council and the winning bidder.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unsuccessful bids

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must return the fee, rent and bonus
  included in each unsuccessful bid to the person that submitted it.

#### [Call for Proposals Process]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1139955 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Call for proposals

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
soliciting interest in rights or interests in First Nation lands, either
the council, or the Minister jointly with the council, may make a call
for proposals. The call may be made by public notice or by other means
and must include the following information:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the type of subsurface contract to be granted;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands to be included in the
  contract area and the oil and gas rights or interests to be granted;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions of the contract, other than
  those set out in these Regulations;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the elements that will be considered in evaluating
  the proposals;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the proposals that are received will
  form the basis for negotiations with the council and the Minister; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} a statement that, in addition to the terms and
  conditions negotiated, the contract will include the terms and
  conditions set out in these Regulations.

#### [Negotiation Process]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1139964 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for subsurface contract

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may apply to the Minister for a subsurface contract that
  confers oil and gas rights or interests in one or more zones in First
  Nation lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary negotiation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before applying for a subsurface contract, an
  applicant and the council must agree on the following terms and
  conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the type of subsurface contract to be applied for;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands to be included in the
    contract area and the oil and gas rights or interests to be granted;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the bonus to be paid;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the initial and intermediate terms of the permit or
    the term of the lease, as the case may be;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a permit, the earning provisions for
    the initial term, including the drilling commitment and deadline for
    completion, the target zone or depth to which each earning well must
    be drilled and a description of the lands to be earned by each; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the royalty to be paid, if it differs from the
    royalty provided for in these Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application to the Minister must be in the
  prescribed form, set out the terms and conditions negotiated by the
  applicant and the council and be accompanied by the subsurface
  contract application fee set out in Schedule 1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any information that is disclosed during the
  negotiations referred to in subsection (2) or in an application
  referred to in subsection (3) must be kept confidential.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of approval

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must not approve the application unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the lands and oil and gas rights or interests
    described in the application have been surrendered or designated
    under section 38 of the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the proposed bonus reflects the fair value of the
    rights or interests to be granted, determined in accordance with
    section 38 of these Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the application is approved, the Minister must
  prepare the subsurface contract and send a copy to the applicant and
  the council. The Minister must fix and include in the contract the
  surface rates to be paid under any related surface contract and the
  seismic rates to be paid under any related exploration licence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criteria --- rates

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The surface rates must be fixed in accordance with
  subsections 73(2) and (3). The seismic rates must be comparable to
  seismic rates for exploration on lands, excluding provincial Crown
  lands, that are similar in size, character and use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal of application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the application is not approved, the Minister must
  send the applicant and the council a notice of refusal that sets out
  the reasons for the refusal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting of contract

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must grant the contract if he or she receives the following
  within 90 days after the day on which a copy of the contract has been
  received by both the applicant and the council:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written resolution of the council approving the
    terms and conditions of the contract and stating that the council
    has chosen to have the rights or interests described in the contract
    granted by way of negotiation rather than public tender;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the bonus and first year's rent; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} two original copies of the contract --- as well as
    an original copy for each future contract holder --- all of which
    are signed by each of them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The contract takes effect on the day on which it is
  granted, unless it provides otherwise.

### [Terms and Conditions of Subsurface Contracts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1139999 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights conferred by contract

**[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** A subsurface contract
holder has the exclusive right to exploit the oil and gas in the lands
in the contract area, to treat that oil, to process that gas and to
dispose of that oil and gas.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial term of permit

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  lands in a permit area are located in a province set out in column 1
  of the table to Schedule 2 and in a region set out in column 2, the
  initial term of the permit is the term set out in column 3. Otherwise,
  the initial term is five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}More than one region

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the lands in a permit area are located in more
  than one region set out in column 2 of the table to Schedule 2, the
  initial term is the term for the region in which the greatest portion
  of the lands is located. If the portion of lands in each region is the
  same, the initial term is the longer of the terms set out in column 3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intermediate term of permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The intermediate term of a permit is three years.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of lease

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** The term of an oil and
gas lease is three years.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term --- exception

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections 48(1) and (2)
  and section 49, with the consent of the applicant and the council, the
  Minister may fix the initial term of a permit or the term of a lease
  at a number of years that is greater than the number established by
  those provisions, to a maximum of five years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amended term

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} With the consent of the holder, the term of a
  subsurface contract may be amended, in accordance with subsection
  20(1), to a maximum of five years.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual rent

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** The annual rent for a
subsurface contract is \$5 per hectare or \$100, whichever is greater.

### [Selection of Lands for Intermediate Term of Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140018 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lands earned

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permit holder earns lands, and may select from those lands for the
  intermediate term of the permit, if, during the initial term, they
  have, in accordance with the earning provisions of their permit,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} drilled a new well in the permit area; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} re-entered an existing well in the permit area and
    drilled at least 150 m of new wellbore.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with earning
  provisions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a holder fails to meet a deadline set out in an
  earning provision of their permit, the permit terminates on the day of
  the deadline with respect to all lands that have not been earned on or
  before that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Selection of lands

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder that has earned lands may select from those
  lands down to the base of the deepest zone into which they have
  drilled, as identified in accordance with Schedule 3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constraints on selection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The lands selected under subsection (3) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be contiguous, if their configuration permits; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include the entire spacing unit in which the
    earning well is located.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Area less than 75%

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permit holder that has drilled a well in a spacing unit whose area is
  composed of less than 75% First Nation lands may select only lands in
  the section in which the well is located, down to the base of the
  deepest zone into which they have drilled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduced earnings --- new well

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder that has drilled a new well, but has not
  drilled to the extent required by the earning provisions of their
  permit, may select only lands in the section in which the well is
  located, down to the base of the deepest zone into which they have
  drilled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduced earnings --- re-entered well

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder that has re-entered and completed a well,
  but has not drilled to the extent referred to in paragraph 52(1)(b)
  and the earning provisions of their permit, may select only lands in
  the spacing unit in which the well is completed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for approval

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  holder that wants a grant of oil and gas rights or interests for the
  intermediate term of their permit must apply to the Minister for
  approval of their selection of lands before the day on which the
  initial term of the permit expires or

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the permit has terminated under subsection
    52(2), within 15 days after the day referred to in that subsection;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the deadline for applying has been extended
    under subsection 62(2), before the extension expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Late application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A holder that fails to apply within the relevant
  deadline referred to in subsection (1) may apply for approval if the
  application is submitted within 15 days after the deadline and is
  accompanied by a late application fee of \$5,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application must be in the prescribed form and
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an identification and description of each well that
    has been drilled and each well that has been re-entered and
    completed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands, including the zones,
    selected for the intermediate term of the permit; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the rent for the first year of the intermediate
    term.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Information about a well that is drilled, or
  re-entered and completed, within 30 days before the relevant deadline
  may be submitted up to 15 days after that deadline, unless the holder
  has received an extension under subsection 62(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receiving an application, the Minister must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} approve the selection of lands if the requirements
    of section 52 are met; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} grant the holder the oil and gas rights or
    interests in the selected lands for the intermediate term of the
    permit if the holder has complied with the requirements of the Act,
    these Regulations and their permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to holder and council

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the selection is approved and the oil and gas
  rights or interests are granted, the Minister must send the holder and
  the council a notice of the approval and a description of the lands,
  including the zones, selected for the intermediate term of the permit.
  If the selection is not approved, the Minister must send the holder a
  notice of refusal that sets out the reasons for the refusal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision

**[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** Sections 47 to 54 do not
apply to a contract that was granted under the [Indian Oil and Gas
Regulations, 1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753).

### [Bitumen Recovery Project Approval]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140061 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for approval

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  subsurface contract holder may apply to the Minister for approval of a
  bitumen recovery project if they have achieved the minimum level of
  evaluation and have applied to the provincial authority for approval
  of the project.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum level of evaluation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The minimum level of evaluation is achieved when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one well is drilled on each section of the lands
    that are subject to the contract --- if the section is in the
    proposed bitumen recovery project area --- and at least 25% of those
    wells are cored; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one well is drilled on at least 60% of the sections
    of the lands that are subject to the contract --- if the sections
    are in the proposed bitumen recovery project area --- at least 25%
    of those wells are cored and seismic data are obtained over at least
    3.2 km in each undrilled section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application for approval of a bitumen recovery project must be in the
  prescribed form and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands to be included in the
    project;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} evidence that the minimum level of evaluation has
    been achieved;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement that the subsurface contract holder has
    applied for or received the provincial authority's approval of the
    project;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the results of an environmental review of the
    project that has been conducted by a qualified environmental
    professional who deals with the holder at arm's length;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions respecting the royalty to
    be paid for the oil and gas recovered from lands in the project
    area;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the reporting requirements for the project;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a detailed description of the project, including
    its location, size and scope, the operations to be carried out, the
    schedule of pre-construction, construction and start-up operations
    and the reasons for selecting that schedule;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a map indicating all the rights and interests in
    the lands in the project area and in any area that is likely to be
    affected by project operations;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an aerial photographic mosaic of the project area
    at a scale that is adequate to show the location of the project
    components, including wells, facilities, tanks, access roads,
    railways, pipelines, public utility corridors, tailings ponds and
    waste storage sites;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a detailed description of storage and
    transportation facilities for the oil and gas, including the size of
    any pipeline that may be used and the name of the entity that owns
    it;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated rate of production of the oil and
    gas for the period for which approval is sought;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the year and month in which the annual minimum
    level of production of bitumen will be achieved;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} a description of the energy sources to be used and
    their anticipated quantity and cost, along with a comparison to
    alternative sources; and

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} the term of the approval sought, along with the
    anticipated starting and completion dates of the project.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The results of the environmental review of the
  bitumen recovery project must be submitted in the prescribed form and
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a site evaluation that is based on the site's
    topography, soils, vegetation, wildlife, sources of water, existing
    structures, archeological and cultural resources, traditional
    ecological knowledge, current land uses and any other feature of the
    site that could be affected by the project;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the operations to be carried out
    during the project, the duration of each and its location on the
    site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the short-term and long-term
    effects that each operation could have on the environment of the
    site and on any surrounding areas;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the proposed mitigation measures,
    the potential residual effects after mitigation and the significance
    of those effects; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of the consultations undertaken with
    the council and the First Nation members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental protection measures
  letter

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} After reviewing the application, the Minister must
  send the applicant and the council a letter that sets out the
  environmental protection measures that must be implemented to permit
  the subsurface contract holder to carry out operations under the
  project.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must approve the bitumen recovery project if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has achieved the minimum level of
    evaluation of the lands in the project area;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a written resolution of the council approving the
    project has been submitted;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the application meets the requirements of
    subsections 57(1) and (2);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the project has been approved by the provincial
    authority; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the project can be carried out without causing
    irremediable damage to the First Nation lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms and conditions of approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The approval may include any terms and conditions
  that are necessary to permit the Minister to verify the progress of
  operations carried out under the project, payment of the approved
  royalty and implementation and compliance with the environmental
  protection measures.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surface contract required

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  operations under a bitumen recovery project must not begin until the
  subsurface contract holder has obtained the surface contracts required
  by these Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with measures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The holder must ensure that all environmental
  protection measures included in the approval are implemented and
  complied with.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum level of production

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  annual minimum level of production of bitumen from the lands that are
  subject to a bitumen recovery project is equal to an average of 2 400
  m^3^ per section in the project area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensation --- bitumen

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the annual minimum level of production of bitumen
  from the lands that are subject to the bitumen recovery project is not
  achieved in any year following the month in which that level was to be
  achieved, the subsurface contract holder must pay compensation equal
  to 25% of the difference between the value of the minimum level of
  production and the value of the actual level of production.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed price

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of calculating the compensation, the
  price of bitumen is deemed to be the monthly Bitumen Floor Price
  published by the Alberta provincial authority for the relevant time
  period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply if the lands that are
  subject to the bitumen recovery project are the subject of an
  authorization under section 42 of the [Indian Oil and Gas Regulations,
  1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional lands, wells or facilities

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** Once a bitumen recovery
project has been approved, the subsurface contract holder must obtain
the approval of the Minister and the council before adding lands, wells
or facilities to the project.

### [Drilling Over Expiry]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140121 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for extension

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  subsurface contract holder may apply to the Minister, in the
  prescribed form, for an extension of the deadline for applying for
  approval of a selection of lands under subsection 54(1) or for
  continuation under section 64 if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the holder has spudded or re-entered a well for the
    purpose of deepening it or completing a new zone, but cannot finish
    the operation before the relevant term expires;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the application is submitted before the relevant
    term expires;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the application identifies the well and indicates
    when it was spudded or re-entered; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the application includes the rent for the following
    year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of extension

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an application is submitted in accordance with
  subsection (1), the Minister must extend the deadline for applying for
  approval of a selection of lands or for continuation to the 30th day
  after the day on which the spudded or re-entered well is rig-released.
  The Minister must send the council a notice of the extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights during extension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} During an extension, the holder may continue to
  produce from any wells in the contract area that are already
  producing, but must not spud or re-enter any other wells in that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to a permit or lease granted
  under the [Indian Oil and Gas Regulations,
  1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753).

### [Continuation of Subsurface Contracts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140135 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifying lands

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  subsurface contract may be continued with respect to the zones,
  identified in accordance with Schedule 4, that are in a spacing unit

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that contains a productive well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is subject, in whole or in part, to a unit
    agreement that includes lands in which a productive well is located,
    or to an oil or gas storage agreement that has been approved by the
    provincial authority;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that is subject to a bitumen recovery project that
    has been approved by the Minister;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} that is subject to a project, other than a bitumen
    recovery project, that has been approved by the provincial authority
    and includes lands in which a productive well is located;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which an offset notice has been
    received in the six months before the day on which the application
    for continuation is submitted or in respect of which a compensatory
    royalty is being paid;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} that is not producing but is shown by mapping to be
    capable of producing from the same pool from which a well on an
    adjoining spacing unit is productive; or

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} that is potentially productive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Horizontal and deviated wells

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), each spacing unit
  from which a horizontal well or deviated well is productive is deemed
  to contain a productive well.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Potentially productive

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of paragraph (1)(g), a spacing unit
  is potentially productive if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it contains a well that is in a mapped pool, is
    neither productive nor abandoned and
