    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} was previously producing, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} contains evidence of the presence of
      hydrocarbons whose potential productivity has not been
      conclusively determined;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it contains an abandoned well and any zone
    penetrated by the well has remaining oil or gas reserves; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it has not been drilled, there is evidence that it
    may be part of a productive pool and it is within a quarter-section
    in the case of oil --- or a section in the case of gas --- that
    adjoins any spacing unit referred to in paragraphs (1)(a) to (e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for continuation

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application for the continuation of a subsurface contract may be made
  to the Minister before the day on which the lease or the intermediate
  term of the permit expires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must be in the prescribed form and
  include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands, including the zones,
    with respect to which continuation is sought;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an indication of the basis for continuation under
    subsection 63(1) along with evidence of that basis; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the rent for the first year of the continuation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  receiving an application for continuation, the Minister must determine
  which lands described in the application are in a spacing unit
  referred to in any of paragraphs 63(1)(a) to (e) and must continue the
  contract with respect to those lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offer to continue

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister determines that lands described in
  the application are in a spacing unit referred to in paragraph
  63(1)(f) or (g), he or she must send the holder an offer to continue
  the contract with respect to those lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must continue the contract with respect
  to lands in a spacing unit referred to in paragraph 63(1)(f) or (g)
  if, within 30 days after the day on which the offer of continuation is
  received, the holder pays the Minister a bonus equal to the greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} \$2,000, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} \$400 for each full or partial legal subdivision
    or, if the lands have not been divided into legal subdivisions,
    \$400 for each unit of land equivalent to 16 hectares, rounded up to
    the nearest whole number of units.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to holder and council

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the holder and the council a
  notice of his or her determination and --- if the contract is
  continued --- a description of the lands, including the zones, with
  respect to which it is continued as well as the basis for
  continuation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights before determination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Before notice of the Minister's determination is
  received, the holder may continue to produce from any wells in the
  contract area that are already producing, but must not spud or
  re-enter any other wells in that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refund

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the contract is not continued, the Minister must
  refund the rent submitted with the application. If the contract is
  continued only in part, the Minister must refund the rent for the
  lands with respect to which the contract is not continued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation requested by council

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may continue, for a maximum period of five years, a contract
  in respect of lands for which continuation was not granted under
  subsection 65(1) or lands for which continuation was granted under
  subsection 65(3) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the council requests the Minister to do so in a
    written resolution sent to the Minister that describes the lands,
    including the zones, to which the request relates and the requested
    period of continuation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a request for continuation under this subsection
    has not previously been made in respect of those lands;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the written consent of the holder is sent to the
    Minister;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the resolution and consent are sent

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a contract in respect of lands for
      which continuation was not granted under subsection 65(1), within
      30 days after the day on which the notice referred to in
      subsection 65(4) is received, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a contract in respect of lands
      for which continuation was granted under subsection 65(3), within
      30 days after the day on which the continuation expires; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the holder has paid the rent for the first year of
    the continuation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional bonus

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister determines that an additional bonus
  must be paid to reflect the fair value, determined in accordance with
  section 38, of the rights or interests to be continued, the Minister
  must not continue the contract unless that additional bonus is paid.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to apply for continuation

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  holder has not applied for continuation before the deadline referred
  to in subsection 64(1), the Minister must determine, as soon as the
  circumstances permit and on the basis of the information in his or her
  possession, whether their contract is eligible for continuation under
  any of paragraphs 63(1)(a) to (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of eligibility

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the contract is eligible for continuation, the
  Minister must send the holder a notice that includes the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands, including the zones,
    with respect to which the contract is eligible for continuation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the basis for continuation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the requirements for an application for
    continuation, as well as the deadline for submission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for continuation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder that has received a notice of eligibility
  may, within 30 days after the day on which the notice is received,
  apply to the Minister, in the prescribed form, for continuation of the
  contract with respect to any of the lands described in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The application must include a description of the
  lands, including the zones, with respect to which continuation is
  sought, the rent for the first year of the continuation and a late
  application fee of \$5,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation to be granted

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the holder pays the required rent and fee, the
  Minister must continue the contract with respect to the lands
  described in the application and send the holder and the council a
  notice of the continuation that describes the lands, including the
  zones, with respect to which it is continued as well as the basis for
  continuation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indefinite continuation

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  contract that is continued on the basis of any of paragraphs 63(1)(a)
  to (e) continues so long as the lands that are subject to the contract
  continue to be eligible on that basis or until the contract is
  surrendered or cancelled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation for one year

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A contract that is continued under subsection 65(3)
  continues for a period of one year after the day on which the contract
  would have expired had it not been continued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-productivity --- oil and gas

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  contract that is continued in respect of lands on the basis of
  paragraph 63(1)(a), (b), (d) or (e) ceases to be eligible for
  continuation on that basis, the Minister must send the holder a notice
  of non-productivity that describes those lands and indicates the basis
  on which the contract has ceased to be eligible.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-productivity --- expiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A contract referred to in subsection (1) expires with
  respect to the lands described in the notice of non-productivity one
  year after the day on which the notice is received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-productivity --- continuation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before the expiry of a contract with respect to lands
  described in a non-productivity notice, the holder may apply under
  section 64 to have the contract continued with respect to those lands
  on the basis of any of paragraphs 63(1)(a) to (e) other than the basis
  mentioned in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for continuation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Before the expiry of a contract continued under
  subsection 65(3) or section 66, the holder may apply under section 64
  to have the contract continued on the basis of any of paragraphs
  63(1)(a) to (e).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inadequate productivity --- bitumen

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In the
  case of a contract continued under paragraph 63(1)(c), if the annual
  minimum level of production of bitumen from the lands that are subject
  to the bitumen recovery project is not achieved in any three years,
  whether or not the years are consecutive, the Minister must send the
  holder a notice of inadequate productivity with respect to those
  lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination and expiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the annual minimum level of production of bitumen
  from the lands that are subject to the bitumen recovery project is not
  achieved in any year following the day on which the notice of
  inadequate productivity is received,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the project terminates on the final day of that
    year; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the contract expires on the final day of that year,
    unless it is continued under subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's determination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When the Minister becomes aware that the annual
  minimum level of production of bitumen from the lands that are subject
  to a bitumen recovery project will not be achieved in a year and the
  contract may expire under paragraph (2)(b), he or she must determine,
  as soon as the circumstances permit and on the basis of the
  information in his or her possession, whether the contract is eligible
  for continuation under any of paragraphs 63(1)(a), (b), (d) or (e)
  and, if so, must continue the contract on that basis.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision --- continuation

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 63 to 68 apply to the
  continuation of any subsurface lease that was granted under the
  [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5) or the Act before these Regulations came
  into force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision ---
  non-productivity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 69 applies to a subsurface lease that was
  continued under the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5) or the Act before
  these Regulations came into force if the lands in the lease area cease
  to be eligible for continuation on the basis on which the lease was
  continued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision --- inadequate
  productivity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Section 70 does not apply if the lands that are
  subject to the bitumen recovery project are the subject of an
  authorization under section 42 of the [Indian Oil and Gas Regulations,
  1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753).

## [Surface Rights or Interests]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140234 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may carry out surface operations on First Nation lands for the
  purpose of exploiting oil and gas if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of operations that require crossing
    over or going through First Nation lands, they hold a right-of-way
    in those lands; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of operations that require the
    exclusive occupation and use of the surface of First Nation lands,
    they hold a surface lease in respect of those lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of entry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person that intends to apply for a surface contract
  in respect of First Nation lands to carry out operations referred to
  in subsection (1) may, with the authorization of the council and any
  First Nation member in lawful possession of those lands, enter on the
  lands to determine the location of proposed facilities, conduct
  surveys and carry out any operation necessary to submit an application
  under section 75.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary negotiation

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before
  applying for a surface contract, the applicant must provide the
  council, and any First Nation member in lawful possession of lands in
  the proposed contract area, with a survey sketch of that area and must
  reach an agreement with them on the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the lands to be included in the contract area;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the operations that will be carried out on those
    lands;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the surface rates, if they have not already been
    fixed by the Minister in a related subsurface contract; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if a service well is to be drilled or an existing
    well is to be used as a service well, the permitted uses of the well
    and the amount of compensation to be paid in respect of the well.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surface rates --- right-of-way

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a right-of-way, the surface rates
  consist of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a right-of-entry charge of \$1,250 per hectare,
    subject to a minimum charge of \$500 and a maximum charge of
    \$5,000; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} initial compensation based on the fair value of
    lands that are similar in size, character and use.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surface rates --- surface lease

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a surface lease, the surface rates
  consist of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the right-of-entry charge referred to in paragraph
    (2)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} initial compensation based on the fair value of
    lands that are similar in size, character and use, the loss of use
    of the lands, adverse effects and general disturbance; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the annual rent for subsequent years, based on the
    loss of use of the lands and adverse effects.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negotiation breakdown

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** If an agreement cannot be
reached on the amount of the initial compensation or annual rent to be
paid, the Minister must, at the request of the applicant, the council or
a First Nation member in lawful possession of lands in the contract
area, determine the amount in accordance with subsection 73(2) or (3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for surface contract

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  application for a surface contract must be submitted to the Minister
  in the prescribed form and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions negotiated with the
    council and any First Nation member in lawful possession of lands in
    the contract area;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a survey plan of the lands to be included in the
    contract area;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the results of an environmental review of the
    operations to be carried out in the contract area that has been
    conducted by a qualified environmental professional who deals with
    the applicant at arm's length; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the surface lease or right-of-way application fee
    set out in Schedule 1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The results of the environmental review must be
  submitted in the prescribed form and include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a site evaluation that is based on the site's
    topography, soils, vegetation, wildlife, sources of water, existing
    structures, archeological and cultural resources, traditional
    ecological knowledge, current land uses and any other feature of the
    site that could be affected by the proposed uses of the lands in the
    contract area;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the operations to be carried out
    on the lands, the duration of each and its location on the site;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the short-term and long-term
    effects that each operation could have on the environment of the
    site and on any surrounding areas;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a description of the proposed mitigation measures,
    the potential residual effects after mitigation and the significance
    of those effects; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of the consultations undertaken with
    the council and the First Nation members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental protection measures

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the application meets the requirements of
  subsection (1) and the proposed operations can be carried out without
  causing irremediable damage to the First Nation lands, the Minister
  must send the applicant and the First Nation a copy of the contract
  that includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the terms and conditions negotiated with the
    council and any First Nation member in lawful possession of lands in
    the contract area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the environmental protection measures that must be
    implemented to permit the holder to carry out operations under the
    contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting of contract

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must grant the contract if he or she
  receives the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} four original copies of the contract, signed by the
    applicant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a written resolution of the council approving the
    contract and the written consent of any First Nation member in
    lawful possession of lands in the contract area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the right-of-entry charge and initial compensation
    owed under the contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with measures

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The holder must ensure that all environmental
  protection measures included in the contract are implemented and
  complied with.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** A surface contract ends
on the day on which its surrender has been approved by the Minister,
unless the contract provides otherwise.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Renegotiation of rent

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  a surface lease provides otherwise, the holder must renegotiate the
  amount of the rent with the Minister, the council and any First Nation
  member in lawful possession of lands in the lease area at the end of
  the shorter of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every five-year period, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any period fixed by the laws of the relevant
    province for the renegotiation of surface leases in lands that are
    not First Nation lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of lease

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must amend the lease to reflect the rent
  renegotiated under subsection (1) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a written resolution of the council approving the
    renegotiated rent is submitted along with the written consent of any
    First Nation member in lawful possession of lands in the lease area;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister determines that the renegotiated rent
    is fair on the basis of the criteria referred to in paragraph
    73(3)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Renegotiation breakdown

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an agreement cannot be reached in renegotiating
  the rent, the Minister must, at the request of the holder, the council
  or any First Nation member in lawful possession of lands in the lease
  area, determine the rent on the basis of the criteria referred to in
  paragraph 73(3)(c) and amend the lease accordingly.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandonment, remediation and reclamation

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** If the lands in a surface
contract area are no longer used for the uses for which the contract was
granted, the holder must abandon any well and facilities in the area and
remediate and reclaim those lands.

## [Royalties]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140300 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of royalty

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as otherwise provided in a special agreement entered into under
  subsection 4(2) of the Act, a subsurface contract holder must pay a
  royalty, in an amount calculated in accordance with Schedule 5, on the
  oil and gas recovered from, or attributed to, lands in the subsurface
  contract area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Index price or actual selling price

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a special agreement entered into under subsection
  4(2) of the Act provides that the royalty on oil or gas is to be
  calculated using a monthly index price or corporate pool price rather
  than the actual selling price, the holder must, in the prescribed
  form, provide the Minister with the index price or corporate pool
  price for each month in which the oil or gas is produced.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline for payment

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** The royalty must be paid
on or before the 25th day of the third month after the month in which
the oil or gas is produced.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royalty --- every sale

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  sale of oil or gas that is recovered from, or attributed to, lands in
  a subsurface contract area must include the sale, on behalf of Her
  Majesty in right of Canada, of any oil or gas that constitutes the
  royalty payable under the Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment in kind

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After giving the contract holder notice, and having
  regard to any obligations that the holder may have in respect of the
  sale of oil or gas, the Minister may, with the prior approval of the
  council, direct the holder to pay all or part of the royalty in kind
  for a specified period or until the Minister directs otherwise.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping of information

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person that produces, sells, acquires or stores oil or gas that has
  been recovered from First Nation lands, or acquires a right to such
  oil or gas, must keep, for a period of 10 years, all information that
  may be used to calculate the royalty owing in respect of that oil and
  gas, including any information required by this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information --- royalties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person referred to in subsection (1) must submit
  the following information to the Minister in the prescribed form as
  soon as it becomes available:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the volume and quality of the oil or gas produced,
    sold, acquired or stored, or to which a right was acquired, by that
    person during the month in which the oil or gas was produced;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the value for which the oil or gas, or a right to
    the oil or gas, was sold or acquired;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any costs and allowances to be taken into account
    in determining the royalty payable on the oil or gas; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other information that is required to calculate
    or verify the royalty payable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information --- related parties

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may require a person referred to in
  subsection (1) to submit information for the purpose of determining
  whether the parties to a transaction are related parties.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Related parties

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (3), persons are
  related parties if they are [[related persons]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
  [[affiliated persons]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or associated
  corporations within the meaning of subsection 251(2), section 251.1
  and subsection 256(1), respectively, of the [Income Tax
  Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to submit plan or diagram

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of verifying the royalty payable under a contract, the
  Minister may order an operator to submit a plan or diagram, drawn to a
  specified scale, of any facility that is used by the operator in
  exploiting oil or gas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An operator that receives an order must submit the
  requested plan or diagram within 30 days after the day on which the
  order is received.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to submit documents

- **[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of verifying the royalty payable under a contract, the
  Minister may send a notice requiring any person that has sold,
  purchased or swapped oil or gas recovered from First Nation lands to
  provide any of the following documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a signed copy of any written sales contract or, if
    the contract was unwritten, a document that sets out its terms and
    conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a transaction statement, invoice or other document
    that sets out the details of the transaction;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any agreement between persons respecting the costs
    and allowances to be taken into account in determining the royalty
    payable on the oil or gas.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deadline

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person that receives a notice sent under
  subsection (1) must submit the requested documents within 14 days
  after the day on which the notice is received.

## [First Nation Audits and Examinations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140339 .Part}

### [General Rules]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140340 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement required

- **[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  First Nation may conduct an audit or examination for the purpose of
  verifying the royalties payable on oil or gas recovered from its lands
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its council has entered into an audit or
    examination agreement with the Minister; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the audit or examination is conducted in accordance
    with the agreement and these Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure to obtain agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A council that has obtained preliminary approval of
  an audit or examination under section 89 may request that the Minister
  enter into an audit or examination agreement under section 90.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who conducts an audit or examination under the Act must have
  the credentials and experience required to carry out their role in the
  audit or examination in accordance with generally accepted auditing
  standards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who conducts an audit or examination under
  the Act, or accompanies an auditor or examiner,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must not be employed by, be affiliated with or
    represent the oil or gas company that is the subject of the audit or
    examination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} must have the certifications and comply with the
    occupational health and safety requirements required or imposed by
    the contract holder or by law; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} must keep confidential any documents or information
    they obtain in connection with the audit or examination and must
    comply with the security requirements imposed by the contract holder
    or by law.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality --- First Nation

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  First Nation that conducts an audit or examination must keep
  confidential any documents or information it obtains in connection
  with the audit or examination and must comply with the security
  requirements imposed by the contract holder or by law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), the council must provide the
  Minister with a copy of all audit or examination reports and working
  papers within 30 days after the day on which the audit or examination
  is completed.

### [Preliminary Approval]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140361 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for preliminary approval

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** To obtain preliminary
approval of an audit or examination, a council must apply to the
Minister in the prescribed form. The application must include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the person whose documents and
  information are to be audited or examined;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name and location of each facility in which the
  audit or examination will be conducted and the name of the facility's
  operator;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the type of audit or examination to be conducted;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the period to be covered by the audit or examination;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the anticipated dates for starting and completing the
  audit or examination;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the reasons that the council believes that the audit
  or examination is necessary; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a statement indicating whether the council is
  prepared to cover the costs of the audit or examination.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must give preliminary approval if the requirements of section
  88 are met, except in the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reasons provided by the council for conducting
    the audit or examination do not establish the existence of a risk
    that warrants an audit or examination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} within the three years before the date of the
    application, the requested type of audit or examination has been
    conducted under the Act in respect of the same contract for the same
    period and the holder was found to be in compliance with the
    contract, these Regulations and the Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the audit or examination is not on the Minister's
    list of priority audits or examinations and the council is not
    prepared to cover its costs; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister and the council do not agree on the
    type of audit or examination to be conducted, the period to be
    covered or the dates for starting and completion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the council notice of his or
  her decision and, if preliminary approval is refused, the reasons for
  the refusal.

### [Request for Agreement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140380 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for agreement

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** A council's request for
an audit or examination agreement must be made to the Minister in the
prescribed form within 180 days after the day on which the notice of
preliminary approval is received and must include

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the proposed auditor or examiner;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a detailed audit or examination plan;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the dates for starting and completing the audit or
  examination;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the name of any person who will accompany the
  proposed auditor or examiner and a description of their role in the
  audit or examination; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} evidence that the proposed auditor or examiner has
  the credentials and experience referred to in subsection 86(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may refuse
the request only if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the information required by section 90 has not been
  provided;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a requirement referred to in section 86 has not been
  complied with; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} one or more circumstances that justified the
  preliminary approval of the audit or examination have changed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement

**[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** If the request is
approved, the Minister must enter into an agreement with the council
that includes the information referred to in paragraphs 88(a) to (d) and
90(a) to (d).

## [Equitable Production of Oil and Gas]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140395 .Part}

### [Holder's Obligations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140396 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensatory royalty

- **[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  subsurface contract holder is obliged to pay Her Majesty in right of
  Canada, in trust for the relevant First Nation, a compensatory royalty
  in respect of each triggering well located in an external spacing unit
  that adjoins a First Nation spacing unit that is in their contract
  area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Royalty for each spacing unit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A compensatory royalty must be paid in respect of
  each First Nation spacing unit in the contract area that adjoins the
  spacing unit in which the triggering well is located.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Beginning of obligation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The obligation to pay the compensatory royalty begins
  on the first day of the month that follows the day on which the offset
  period ends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offset period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The offset period begins on the day on which an
  offset notice is received and ends on the 180th day after that day or

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the offset notice is not sent until after
    confidential information about the well is made public, the 90th day
    after that day; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the offset period has been extended under
    paragraph 5(1)(d) of the Act, the day on which the extension
    expires.

### [Offset Notice]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140408 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offset notice

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  Minister becomes aware of the existence of a triggering well, the
  Minister must send an offset notice to every subsurface contract
  holder that is obliged to pay a compensatory royalty under section 93.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of contract

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If any lands in a First Nation spacing unit that
  adjoins a spacing unit in which a triggering well is located are not
  subject to a subsurface contract, the Minister must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} send the council a notice of the existence of the
    triggering well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send an offset notice to any person that becomes a
    subsurface lease holder in respect of those lands; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} send an offset notice to any person that becomes a
    permit holder in respect of those lands one year after the effective
    date of the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, on the day on which an offset notice is required
  to be sent, any information about a triggering well is confidential
  under the laws of the relevant province, the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must send, to every contract holder to which the
    offset notice will be sent, a notice of the existence of the
    triggering well and the information set out in paragraphs 95(1)(a)
    and (c) in respect of that well; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} must not send the offset notice until the Minister
    becomes aware that the confidential information has been made
    public.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information included in notice

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  offset notice must include the following information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the subsurface contract holder, the
    contract number and the holder's percentage share in the contract;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the lands in the contract area
    that are subject to the notice;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the unique well identifier of the triggering well;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the area of the First Nation lands in the spacing
    unit in which the triggering well is located, expressed as a
    percentage of the area of that spacing unit;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a description of the external spacing unit in which
    the triggering well is located and the offset zone;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a horizontal or multilateral
    triggering well, the total length of the well, the total length of
    the horizontal section of the well and the length of the section of
    the well that is producing from the external spacing unit;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a deviated well that is producing
    from more than one spacing unit, the total length of the well and
    the length of the section of the well that is producing from the
    external spacing unit;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the offset period; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} statements indicating that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the spacing unit in which the triggering well is
      located adjoins the First Nation spacing unit in the contract area
      described in paragraph (b),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the obligation to pay a compensatory royalty
      begins on the first day of the month that follows the day on which
      the offset period ends,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the compensatory royalty must be paid on or
      before the 25th day of the third month after the month in which
      the obligation to pay it begins and, subsequently, on or before
      the 25th day of each subsequent month, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the obligation to pay the compensatory royalty
      ends in any of the circumstances set out in subsection 100(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the council a copy of the
  offset notice and, when the offset period ends, a notice indicating
  that the holder's obligation to pay a compensatory royalty has begun.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No obligation

- **[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  obligation to pay a compensatory royalty does not begin if, during the
  offset period, the subsurface contract holder submits to the Minister
  information that establishes any of the following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the triggering well is not draining from the offset
    zone referred to in the offset notice;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offset zone of the triggering well has been
    abandoned, as shown in the records of the provincial authority;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an offset well is producing from the offset zone;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the spacing unit in which the triggering well is
    located no longer adjoins the First Nation spacing unit referred to
    in the offset notice;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the offset zone in the First Nation spacing unit is
    subject to a unit agreement under which oil or gas is being or is
    deemed to be produced;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the triggering well is subject to a storage
    agreement that has been approved by the provincial authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to holder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After determining whether a circumstance set out in
  subsection (1) has been established, the Minister must send the holder
  a notice of his or her determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surrender

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A holder is not obliged to pay a compensatory royalty
  if, during the offset period, they surrender their rights or interests
  down to the base of the offset zone in the spacing unit to which the
  offset notice applies, except for any rights or interests in a zone
  from which a well is productive or that is subject to a unit agreement
  or to a storage agreement that has been approved by the provincial
  authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the holder has established a circumstance set out
  in subsection (1) or has surrendered their rights or interests under
  subsection (3), the Minister must send the council a notice indicating
  that the holder's obligation to pay a compensatory royalty is relieved
  and the reasons that it is relieved.

### [Calculation and Payment of Compensatory Royalty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140454 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensatory royalty

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  monthly compensatory royalty that is payable by a subsurface contract
  holder is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vertical triggering well or
    deviated triggering well that is producing from a single spacing
    unit, an amount equal to the amount that would have been payable by
    the holder as a royalty for that month if the triggering well were
    producing from the adjoining First Nation spacing unit that is in
    their contract area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a horizontal triggering well,
    multilateral triggering well or deviated triggering well that is
    producing from more than one spacing unit, an amount equal to the
    percentage, calculated in accordance with the following formula, of
    the amount referred to in paragraph (a):

    ::: {#1140460 .Paragraph}
    (L/T) × 100

    where

    [L]{.dfn}
    :   is the length of the section of the triggering well that is
        located in the adjoining external spacing unit and is capable of
        producing oil or gas from the offset zone, and

    [T]{.dfn}
    :   is the total length of the section of the well that is capable
        of producing oil or gas.
    :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prorated amount

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the triggering well is located in an external
  spacing unit that contains First Nation lands, the monthly
  compensatory royalty that is payable is an amount calculated in
  accordance with the formula

  ::: {#1140466 .Subsection}
  C × (100 -- I)/100

  where

  [C]{.dfn}
  :   is the amount of the compensatory royalty that is payable under
      subsection (1); and

  [I]{.dfn}
  :   is the area of the First Nation lands in the spacing unit,
      expressed as a percentage of the area of that spacing unit.
  :::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of compensatory royalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of calculating the monthly
  compensatory royalty,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the volume of oil, gas or condensate to be used in
    the royalty formula is the volume of oil, raw gas or condensate that
    was produced in the month by the triggering well, as shown by the
    records of the provincial authority; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the price to be used, in respect of that month, is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of oil, in Saskatchewan, the price
      indicated in the Monthly Crude Oil Royalty/Tax Factor History,
      published by the Ministry of Energy and Resources, and, in the
      other provinces, the monthly par price for light, medium, heavy or
      ultra heavy oil, as the case may be, published by Alberta's
      Department of Energy,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of gas, in Saskatchewan, the price
      indicated in the Monthly Natural Gas Royalty/Tax Factor History,
      published by the Ministry of Energy and Resources, and, in the
      other provinces, the Gas Reference Price in the monthly
      information letter Natural Gas Royalty Prices and Allowances,
      published by Alberta's Department of Energy, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of condensate, the Pentanes Plus
      Reference Price in the monthly information letter Natural Gas
      Royalty Prices and Allowances, published by Alberta's Department
      of Energy.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compensatory royalty --- confidential
  well

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an offset notice sent under paragraph
  94(3)(b), the month referred to in paragraph (3)(a) for the first
  monthly compensatory royalty is the month whose first day follows the
  period that begins on the day on which the information sent under
  paragraph 94(3)(a) is received and ends on the 180th day after that
  day. For each subsequent monthly compensatory royalty, the month is
  each subsequent month.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Heating value

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the royalty calculation requires the conversion of
  a price in dollars per gigajoule (GJ) into a price in dollars per 1000
  m^3^, the heating value is 37.7 GJ/1000 m^3^.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No deduction

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No deduction for costs or allowances is to be made in
  the calculation of the compensatory royalty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transitional provision

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply to a compensatory royalty
  owing under the [Indian Oil and Gas Regulations,
  1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation and payment of compensatory
royalty

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** On or before the 25th day
of the third month after the month in which the obligation to pay the
compensatory royalty begins, and on or before the 25th day of each
subsequent month, the subsurface contract holder must pay the Minister
the monthly compensatory royalty and, in the prescribed form, provide
the information that is required to verify its calculation.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amended spacing unit

**[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** The obligation to pay a
compensatory royalty continues despite any change in the size of the
First Nation spacing unit or the external spacing unit in which the
triggering well is located if the two spacing units remain adjoined.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}End of obligation to pay

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  obligation to pay a compensatory royalty ends if the subsurface
  contract holder

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishes any of the circumstances set out in
    subsection 96(1); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} surrenders their rights or interests down to the
    base of the offset zone in the spacing unit to which the offset
    notice applies, except for any rights or interests in a zone from
    which a well is productive or that is subject to a unit agreement or
    to a storage agreement that has been approved by the provincial
    authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to holder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After determining whether a circumstance set out in
  subsection 96(1) has been established, the Minister must send the
  holder a notice informing them of his or her determination and, if the
  obligation ends, the day on which it ends.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final day of obligation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The obligation to pay a compensatory royalty ends

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the holder sends the Minister a notice
    establishing a circumstance set out in subsection 96(1), on the
    first day of the month in which the Minister receives the notice; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the holder has surrendered their rights or
    interests, on the first day of the month that follows the month in
    which the Minister receives a notice of the surrender.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the obligation to pay a compensatory royalty ends,
  the Minister must send the council a notice indicating that it has
  ended and the reasons that it has ended.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
97(7), sections 93 to 100 and 111 apply to any subsurface contract that
was granted under the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5) or the Act.

### [Offset Wells]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1140504 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to produce

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an offset well fails to produce any oil or gas for three consecutive
  months after the offset period has ended, the subsurface contract
  holder must pay a compensatory royalty in respect of the triggering
  well whose production was to be offset.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Beginning of compensatory royalty
  obligation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The obligation to pay the compensatory royalty begins
  on the first day of the month that follows the three-month period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the council a notice
  indicating that the holder's obligation to pay a compensatory royalty
  has begun.

## [Service Wells]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140512 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prior approval

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  well must not be used as a service well without the prior approval of
  the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application for approval must be in the
  prescribed form, be accompanied by a copy of the provincial
  authority's approval of the service well and include the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a description of the well;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a detailed description of the proposed uses of the
    well and the proposed uses of any related facilities; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the bonus and the annual compensation to be paid
    for any disposal rights.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve the proposed uses of the
  service well if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made in accordance with
    subsection (2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the approval of the council has been obtained; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the approval will benefit the relevant First
    Nation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The contract holder must send the Minister notice of
  any changes in the provincial authority's approval referred to in
  subsection (2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** Section 103 does not
apply to a service well that is part of a project that has been approved
by the provincial authority or a bitumen recovery project that has been
approved by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** Section 103 does not
apply to a disposal rights agreement that was entered into before these
Regulations came into force.

## [Pooling, Production Allocation and Unit Agreements]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140532 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Single spacing unit production

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  well is producing from First Nation lands, the Minister must determine
  the percentage of production from the well to be allocated to each
  contract in the spacing unit from which the well is producing, based
  on the area of the First Nation lands that are subject to each
  contract, in proportion to the area of the spacing unit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to holder and council

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send each holder and the council a
  notice indicating the percentage of the production that is allocated
  to each contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple spacing unit production

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  well is producing from more than one spacing unit and the lands from
  which it is producing are not entirely First Nation lands or are not
  subject to a single contract, the Minister must determine the
  percentage of production from the well to be allocated to the First
  Nation lands and to each contract, based on the criteria used by the
  provincial authority in making such allocations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to holder and council

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send each holder and the council a
  notice indicating the percentage of the production that is allocated
  to the First Nation lands and to each contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unit agreement

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, with the prior approval of the council, enter into a
  unit agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allocation of production

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The calculation of royalties payable under a contract
  that is subject to a unit agreement must be based on the production
  allocated to each tract as specified in the agreement.

## [Surrender, Default and Cancellation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140548 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surrender of subsurface rights or
interests

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  subsurface contract holder may surrender their rights or interests
  under the contract, in whole or in part, by sending the Minister a
  notice of surrender in the prescribed form.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Partial surrender of subsurface rights
  or interests

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In a partial surrender of subsurface rights or
  interests,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} all the rights and interests in a spacing unit must
    be surrendered; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rent for subsequent years is reduced in
    proportion to the reduction of the lands that are subject to the
    contract, to a minimum of \$100.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} When rights or interests under a subsurface contract
  are surrendered, the Minister must send the council a copy of the
  notice of surrender and, in the case of a partial surrender, a copy of
  the amended contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Surrender of surface rights or interests

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  surface contract holder may surrender their rights or interests under
  the contract, in whole or in part, by applying in the prescribed form
  for the Minister's approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to council

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the council a copy of the
  application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must approve the surrender if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the holder is not in default under the contract,
    these Regulations or an order given under the Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister and the council have inspected the
    contract area to be surrendered and the Minister has confirmed that
    the remediation and reclamation of the surface in that area are
    satisfactory; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a partial surrender, the boundaries
    of the remaining contract area continue to meet the requirements of
    these Regulations and the partial surrender approval application fee
    set out in Schedule 1 has been paid.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adjusted rent

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the surrender of rights or interests under a
  surface contract is partial, the rent for subsequent years is reduced
  in proportion to the reduction of the lands that are subject to the
  contract. However, the rent must be no less than the rent payable for
  1.6 hectares.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the surrender of rights or interests under a
  surface contract is approved, the Minister must send the council a
  notice to that effect and, in the case of a partial surrender, a copy
  of the amended contract.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-compliance notice

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  holder fails to comply with their contract, the Act or these
  Regulations, the Minister may send them a notice that identifies the
  non-compliance and warns that the contract will be cancelled if the
  holder is in default.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Response to notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within 30 days after the day on which the notice is
  received, the holder must remedy the non-compliance identified in the
  notice or, if the non-compliance does not relate to money owed under
  the Act, submit to the Minister a plan that shows how and when it will
  be remedied and why the proposed deadline is justified in the
  circumstances. Subsequently, the holder must remedy the non-compliance
  in accordance with the plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deficient plan

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the plan does not meet the requirements of
  subsection (2), the Minister must send the holder a notice to that
  effect that identifies its deficiencies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amended plan

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A holder that receives a notice sent under
  subsection (3) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 30 days after the day on which the notice is
    received, submit to the Minister an amended plan that corrects the
    deficiencies identified in the notice; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} remedy the non-compliance identified in the notice
    sent under subsection (1) in accordance with that plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Default

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A holder that receives a notice sent under
  subsection (1) is in default if they do not comply with the
  requirements of subsection (2) or, if applicable, subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation for default

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must cancel the contract of a holder
  that is in default.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-payment of compensatory royalty

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If a contract is to be cancelled for non-payment of a
  compensatory royalty, the Minister must cancel the rights or interests
  conferred by the contract down to the base of the offset zone in the
  spacing unit to which the offset notice applies, except for any rights
  or interests in a spacing unit referred to in any of paragraphs
  63(1)(a) to (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation notice

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} When a contract is cancelled, the Minister must send
  the holder a notice indicating that their contract is cancelled, the
  reason for the cancellation and its effective date.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the council a copy of every
  notice sent under this section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuing liability

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** When a contract ends,
any liabilities for outstanding amounts that are owed under the
contract, any liabilities for damages caused by operations carried out
under the contract and any obligations respecting abandonment,
remediation or reclamation survive the end of the contract.

## [Administrative Monetary Penalties]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140595 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designated provisions

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** The provisions set out
in Schedule 6 are designated as provisions whose contravention is a
violation that may be proceeded with under sections 22 to 28 of the Act.

## [Transitional Provisions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140598 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Executive Director

**[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** The powers, duties and
functions of the Executive Director under the [Indian Oil and Gas
Regulations, 1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753) are to be exercised or
performed by the Minister and any reference to the Executive Director in
a contract granted under those Regulations is deemed to be a reference
to the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permits

**[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** Sections 15, 16 and 18
to 21 of the [Indian Oil and Gas Regulations,
1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753) continue to apply to permits granted
under those Regulations.

## [Repeal]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140603 .Part}

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** The [Indian Oil and Gas
Regulations, 1995](/eng/regulations/SOR-94-753)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^1^](#fn_1){#fn_1-ID0EBBA} are repealed.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^1^](#fn_1-ID0EBBA){#fn_1}SOR/94-753
  :::

## [Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1140605 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[S.C. 2009, c. 7]{.HistoricalNote}

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_SOR-2019-196_e_transform_hq_25385){#fn_SOR-2019-196_e_transform_hq_25385-ID0EBCA}[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
.sectionLabel}** These Regulations come into force on the day on which
An Act to amend the Indian Oil and Gas Act comes into force, but if they
are registered after that day, they come into force on the day on which
they are registered.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fn_SOR-2019-196_e_transform_hq_25385-ID0EBCA){#fn_SOR-2019-196_e_transform_hq_25385}\[Note:
  Regulations in force August 1, 2019, *see* SI/2019-39.\]
  :::

## [SCHEDULE 2]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsections 48(1) and (2))]{.OriginatingRef}[Initial Term of Permits]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1140618 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  **1** The following definitions apply in this Schedule.

  [[Area 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Area 1]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lands in Area 1 as set
      out in Schedule 2 to the Petroleum and Natural Gas Drilling
      Licence and Lease Regulation, B.C. Reg. 10/82. ([Zone
      1]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Area 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Area 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lands in Area 2 as set
      out in Schedule 2 to the Petroleum and Natural Gas Drilling
      Licence and Lease Regulation, B.C. Reg. 10/82. ([Zone
      2]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Area 3]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Area 3]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lands in Area 3 as set
      out in Schedule 2 to the Petroleum and Natural Gas Drilling
      Licence and Lease Regulation, B.C. Reg. 10/82. ([Zone
      3]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Foothills Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Foothills Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lands in the
      Foothills Region as set out in Schedule 1 to the Petroleum and
      Natural Gas Tenure Regulation, AR 263/1997. ([région des
      contreforts]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Northern Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Northern Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lands in the
      Northern Region as set out in Schedule 1 to the Petroleum and
      Natural Gas Tenure Regulation, AR 263/1997. ([région du
      Nord]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Plains Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Plains Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lands in the
      Plains Region as set out in Schedule 1 to the Petroleum and
      Natural Gas Tenure Regulation, AR 263/1997. ([région des
      plaines]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[township]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[township]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a township laid out in
      accordance with sections 55 to 61 of The Land Surveys Regulations,
      R.S.S. c. L-4.1 Reg 1. ([canton]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

## [SCHEDULE 3]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsections 1(1) and 52(3))]{.OriginatingRef}[Zones --- Intermediate Term]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1140636 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  **1** The following definitions apply in this Schedule.

  [[ILND]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ILND]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the internal limit of a zone,
      whether upper or lower, that is not
      defined. ([LIND]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[KB]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[KB]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means kelly bushing, which serves as
      the point on the rotary drilling table from which downhole well
      log depths are measured. ([FE]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[NDE]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[NDE]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means not deep enough and, in relation
      to a reference well, means that the well was not drilled to a
      depth that was sufficient to penetrate the upper or lower limit of
      a particular zone. ([FI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[NP]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[NP]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means not present and, in relation to a
      zone, means that the zone is not present at the location where the
      reference well was drilled. ([NP]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[TVD]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[TVD]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means true vertical
      depth. ([PVR]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Zones

  - **2** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For each of the First Nation lands set out in
    this Schedule, the lands that may be selected are the zones set out
    in column 1 of the table that correspond to the well log data set
    out in column 2 that match the well log data for the well that was
    drilled or re-entered by the subsurface contract holder.

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple logs

    [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is more than one set of well log data set
    out in column 2 for a zone, the set derived from the reference well
    that is nearest to the earning well must be used to determine the
    zones.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unidentified zone

  **3** If a well is drilled into a zone that is not identified in a
  table to this Schedule, the Minister must determine the upper and
  lower limits of the deepest zone penetrated by the well, based on a
  review of the well log data that relate to other wells in the vicinity
  and on any well log data that are available and relate to lands in the
  vicinity.

::::: section
:::: {#1140916 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE 4]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsections 1(1) and 63(1))]{.OriginatingRef}[Zones --- Continuation]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1140917 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  **1** The following definitions apply in this Schedule.

  [[ILND]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ILND]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the internal limit of a zone,
      whether upper or lower, that is not
      defined. ([LIND]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[KB]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[KB]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means kelly bushing, which serves as
      the point on the rotary drilling table from which downhole well
      log depths are measured. ([FE]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[NDE]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[NDE]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means not deep enough and, in relation
      to a reference well, means that the well was not drilled to a
      depth that was sufficient to penetrate the upper or lower limit of
      a particular zone. ([FI]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[NP]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[NP]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means not present and, in relation to a
      zone, means that the zone is not present at the location where the
      reference well was drilled. ([NP]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[TVD]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[TVD]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means true vertical
      depth. ([PVR]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Zones

  - **2** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a contract that is continued
    on the basis of any of paragraphs 63(1)(a) to (g) or under section
    66 of these Regulations, for each of the First Nation lands set out
    in this Schedule, the zones with respect to which continuation may
    be sought are the zones set out in column 1 of the table that
    correspond to the well log data set out in column 2.

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Multiple logs

    [(2)]{.lawlabel} If there is more than one set of well log data set
    out in column 2 for a zone, the set derived from the reference well
    that is nearest to the relevant spacing unit must be used to
    determine the zones that may be continued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unidentified zone

  **3** If the zone with respect to which the contract may be continued
  is not identified in a table to this Schedule, the Minister must
  determine the upper and lower limits of the relevant zone, based on a
  review of well log data that relate to wells in the vicinity of the
  relevant spacing unit and on any other well log data that are
  available and relate to lands in the vicinity.

::::: section
:::: {#1141197 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE 5]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsection 79(1))]{.OriginatingRef}[Royalties]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-1141198 .scheduleLabel}
:::

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#interpretation .SchedHeadL2}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[marketable
  gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  **1** In this Schedule, [[marketable gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
  gas, consisting mainly of methane, that meets industry or utility
  specifications for use as a domestic, commercial or industrial fuel or
  as an industrial raw material.

#### [Actual Selling Price]{.HTitleText2} {#actual-selling-price .SchedHeadL2}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Highest value

  - **2** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Schedule, if the
    Minister determines that the actual selling price of oil or gas is
    less than the fair value of that oil or gas at the time and place of
    production, the actual selling price is deemed to be that fair
    value. In that case, the Minister must send the contract holder
    notice of the royalties payable and, within 30 days after the day on
    which the notice is received, the holder must pay the royalties
    payable in accordance with that notice.

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to consider

    [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining the fair value of oil or gas, the
    Minister, in consultation with the council, must take into account
    the following factors:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any applicable reference price;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of gas, transportation cost, volume
      of fuel gas and heat value;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of oil, transportation cost, quality
      adjustment for sulphur content and density;

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether the parties to the transaction are
      related parties within the meaning of subsection 82(4) of these
      Regulations;

    - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the Bank of Canada's daily exchange rate for
      converting U.S. dollars to Canadian dollars; and

    - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the factor of 6.2898 to convert barrels of oil to
      cubic metres of oil.

#### [Oil Royalty]{.HTitleText2} {#oil-royalty .SchedHeadL2}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of royalty --- oil

  - **3** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The royalty on oil that is recovered from, or
    attributed to, lands in a contract area consists of the basic
    royalty determined in accordance with subsection (2) or (3) and the
    supplementary royalty determined in accordance with subsection (5).
    All amounts are to be calculated at the time and place of
    production.

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Basic royalty --- first five years

    [(2)]{.lawlabel} During the five-year period beginning on the day on
    which production of oil from the contract area begins, the basic
    royalty for each month of that period is equal to the actual selling
    price multiplied by the monthly royalty determined in accordance
    with column 2 of the table to this subsection, based on the monthly
    production, referred to in column 1, of oil that is recovered from,
    or attributed to, each well.

    <figure id="1141221">
    <table
    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
    <thead>
    <tr class="topdouble">
    <th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
    scope="col">Item</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
    <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Monthly
    Production (m<sup>3</sup>)</th>
    <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Monthly Royalty
    (m<sup>3</sup>)</th>
    </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">80 or less</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">10% of the number of cubic metres</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">More than 80 but not more than
    160</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">8 m<sup>3</sup> plus 20% of the number of
    cubic metres in excess of 80</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight">More than 160</td>
    <td>24 m<sup>3</sup> plus 26% of the number of cubic metres in excess of
    160</td>
    </tr>
    </tbody>
    </table>
    <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
    </figure>

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Basic royalty --- subsequent years

    [(3)]{.lawlabel} Beginning immediately after the period referred to
    in subsection (2), the basic royalty for each subsequent month is
    equal to the actual selling price multiplied by the monthly royalty
    determined in accordance with column 2 of the table to this
    subsection, based on the monthly production, referred to in column
    1, of oil that is recovered from, or attributed to, each well.

    <figure id="1141229">
    <table
    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
    <thead>
    <tr class="topdouble">
    <th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
    scope="col">Item</th>
    <th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
    <th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Monthly
    Production (m<sup>3</sup>)</th>
    <th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Monthly Royalty
    (m<sup>3</sup>)</th>
    </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">80 or less</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">10% of the number of cubic metres</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">More than 80 but not more than
    160</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">8 m<sup>3</sup> plus 20% of the number of
    cubic metres in excess of 80</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">More than 160 but not more than
    795</td>
    <td class="noBorderBottom">24 m<sup>3</sup> plus 26% of the number of
    cubic metres in excess of 160</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
    <td class="noBorderRight">4</td>
    <td class="noBorderRight">More than 795</td>
    <td>189 m<sup>3</sup> plus 40% of the number of cubic metres in excess
    of 795</td>
    </tr>
    </tbody>
    </table>
    <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
    </figure>

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to council

    [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must send the council notice of the
    date on which the production referred to in subsection (2) begins.

  - [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supplementary royalty

    [(5)]{.lawlabel} The supplementary royalty is

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of oil to which subsection (2)
      applies, the amount determined by the formula

      ::: {#1141240 .Paragraph}
      (T -- B)0.50(P -- R)

      where

      [T]{.dfn}
      :   is the amount of oil, in cubic metres, that is recovered from,
          or attributed to, each well in the contract area during the
          month,

      [B]{.dfn}
      :   is the monthly royalty, in cubic metres, determined in
          accordance with the table to subsection (2),

      [P]{.dfn}
      :   is the actual selling price of the oil per cubic metre, and

      [R]{.dfn}
      :   is the reference price, equal to
          - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of oil recovered from a source
            set out in column 2 of the table to this subsection, the
            price set out in column 3, and

          - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, \$25 per cubic metre;
            and
      :::

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of oil to which subsection (3)
      applies, the amount determined by the formula

      ::: {#1141249 .Paragraph}
      (T -- B)\[0.75(P -- R -- \$12.58) + \$6.29\]

      where

      [T]{.dfn}
      :   is the amount of oil, in cubic metres, that is recovered from,
          or attributed to, each well in the contract area during the
          month,

      [B]{.dfn}
      :   is the monthly royalty, in cubic metres, determined in
          accordance with the table to subsection (3),

      [P]{.dfn}
      :   is the actual selling price of the oil per cubic metre, and

      [R]{.dfn}
      :   is the reference price, equal to
          - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of oil recovered from a source
            set out in column 2 of the table to this subsection, the
            price set out in column 3, and

          - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, \$25 per cubic metre.
      :::

    <figure id="1141257">

    class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
